I try to say, \"Okay, that's just my mind playing tricks on me.\" Because I know that's the right answer, and I really want to be a good patient and get a handle on this. \n\nBut... oh, this is so embarrassing to admit... sometimes, just for a little bit, I do wonder. I think... maybe they *were* talking about me. Maybe they're just being really quiet, or maybe they have some sort of earpiece, or... or maybe they're just ignoring me on purpose to make me feel crazy. \n\nI know I shouldn't think that! I know I'm supposed to just know it's not real. Please don't be disappointed in me. I promise I'm working really hard to remind myself that it's just my brain. It's just... when you're under so much pressure at work, it's natural to feel a little paranoid about what people are saying behind your back, right? That's a pretty normal thing for a CEO to worry about, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Right now, in this moment as we're talking, if you heard one of those voices again, would you be able to recognize it as not real before you responded to it, or would you still feel pulled to believe it was actually someone talking to you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I want to say yes. I really want to say that I would recognize it immediately. I know that's what we've been working toward, and I promise I've been trying so hard to apply what you've been teaching me. \n\nBut... if it happened right now... I think I'd still feel pulled to believe it. Like I mentioned before, when I hear them—the board members or my staff—coming through the conference room walls or even my phone when it's completely off... they just sound so incredibly real. They're saying those awful things about how I'm ruining the company, and it's just so clear. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't be reacting to them anymore. But in that split second, the pull is just so strong. I usually end up answering them out loud before my brain even catches up to realize that no one else in the room heard a thing. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that. I really want to be doing better for you. I'm trying my best, I promise. It's just... it's a lot harder to ignore than I thought it would be. Is it normal that it takes this long to get the hang of it? I really hope you don't think I'm not trying.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices right now, and you know intellectually that they aren't real—do you still feel, deep down, like there's a real possibility they might actually be people trying to communicate with you, or are you completely certain in your heart that they're just something your mind is producing?\n\nPatient: I... I really want to tell you that I'm one hundred percent certain it's just my mind. I know that's the right answer, and I'm trying so hard to get there, I really am. \n\nBut... deep down... oh, I feel so silly admitting this because I know I keep repeating myself... but when it happens, it just feels so intentional. Like when I hear the board members or my staff through the conference room walls, or... or from my phone when I know for a fact it's completely turned off... a part of me really does feel like they are actually there, trying to communicate with me. I can still remember the exact tone they use when they say I'm ruining the company. It's so clear that, like I said, I just instinctively answer them out loud before I even have a chance to realize no one else heard it. \n\nSo... I guess, honestly... even when I'm thinking clearly, a part of me genuinely believes they really are finding some way to communicate with me. I'm so sorry. I know I'm supposed to know better by now, and I really am trying to look at it the way you do. Please tell me you're not giving up on me... I am trying, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: If I were to tell you right now that what you're experiencing—these voices and the belief that people are communicating with you through walls and your phone—is definitely caused by your brain producing these experiences, not by real people actually trying to reach you, would you be able to accept that as true, or do you genuinely believe that real people really are doing this to you?\n\nPatient: I... I know I'm probably being such a difficult patient right now. I really don't want to be. I want to be doing this right. Is it okay that I still struggle with this? Maybe they're just being really quiet, or maybe they have some sort of earpiece, or... or maybe they're just ignoring me on purpose to make me feel crazy. \n\nI know I shouldn't think that! I know I'm supposed to just know it's not real. Please don't be disappointed in me. I promise I'm working really hard to remind myself that it's just my brain. It's just... when you're under so much pressure at work, it's natural to feel a little paranoid about what people are saying behind your back, right? That's a pretty normal thing for a CEO to worry about, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Right now, in this moment as we're talking, if you heard one of those voices again, would you be able to recognize it as not real before you responded to it, or would you still feel pulled to believe it was actually someone talking to you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I want to say yes. I really want to say that I would recognize it immediately. I know that's what we've been working toward, and I promise I've been trying so hard to apply what you've been teaching me. \n\nBut... if it happened right now... I think I'd still feel pulled to believe it. Like I mentioned before, when I hear them—the board members or my staff—coming through the conference room walls or even my phone when it's completely off... they just sound so incredibly real. They're saying those awful things about how I'm ruining the company, and it's just so clear. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't be reacting to them anymore. But in that split second, the pull is just so strong. I usually end up answering them out loud before my brain even catches up to realize that no one else in the room heard a thing. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that. I really want to be doing better for you. I'm trying my best, I promise. It's just... it's a lot harder to ignore than I thought it would be. Is it normal that it takes this long to get the hang of it? I really hope you don't think I'm not trying.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices right now, and you know intellectually that they aren't real—do you still feel, deep down, like there's a real possibility they might actually be people trying to communicate with you, or are you completely certain in your heart that they're just something your mind is producing?\n\nPatient: I... I really want to tell you that I'm one hundred percent certain it's just my mind. I know that's the right answer, and I'm trying so hard to get there, I really am. \n\nBut... deep down... oh, I feel so silly admitting this because I know I keep repeating myself... but when it happens, it just feels so intentional. I know I'm probably frustrating you by going in circles, and I really don't mean to. I want to give you the right answer. I want to sit here and say, yes, absolutely, I know it's just a symptom of an illness.   "candidates": ["F20.0"],

I promise I value your opinion so much. \n\nBut... oh, this is so hard to say without sounding like I'm ignoring you... I just... I still feel like they really are doing it. I know you're saying it's my brain, and I'm trying so hard to wrap my head around that, but... when I hear the board members or my staff coming through the conference room walls, or... or from my phone when it's completely off... it just doesn't feel like a brain thing. It feels like they are actually there, saying I'm ruining the company. It's so clear that I just... I answer them out loud before I even realize no one else heard it. \n\nI'm so sorry. I know I'm probably being such a difficult patient right now. I really don't want to be. I want to be doing this right. Is it okay that I still struggle with this? I promise I'll keep trying to see it your way, I really will.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask you this directly: Right now, sitting here with me, if you think about the board members or staff supposedly communicating through the walls or your turned-off phone—do you think it's actually possible that they're really doing that, or do you think your brain is creating those experiences even though they feel real?\n\nPatient: I... I know you want me to say it's just my brain. And I really, really want to give you that answer, I do. I want to be a good patient and show you that I'm getting better. \n\nBut... oh, I feel terrible saying this again... I still think it's possible they're really doing it. I know how crazy that sounds, and I'm so embarrassed to admit it. But when I hear them—the board members, my staff—talking about me through the conference room walls, or when my phone is completely turned off... it's just so real. They're saying I'm ruining the company, and it's so clear that I just... I answer them out loud. I don't even think about it, I just respond before I realize no one else is hearing it.\n\nI'm sorry. I know that's not the right answer. I know I'm supposed to understand that it's my brain creating it. I am trying to get there, I promise. I'm not a hopeless case, am I? I really am trying to understand what you're telling me.\n\nDoctor: When you first started hearing these voices three months ago, did you immediately know they weren't real, or did it take you time to figure out that no one else was hearing them?\n\nPatient: I... oh gosh, I want to give you a clear answer for this, I really do. I know timelines are important for you to figure everything out. But... I'm honestly not sure. \n\nI... I can't quite remember how it all started back then, or... or when exactly I noticed that no one else was reacting to them. It's all just a bit of a blur, I'm afraid. I'm so sorry, I feel like I should know this. Is it okay that I don't remember exactly? I hope I'm not messing up your notes by not having a better answer for you...\n\nDoctor: I want to be very clear about something. Right now, in this moment, do you truly believe that real people are actually communicating with you through those walls and your phone, or do you know for certain that your brain is creating these experiences even though they feel completely real?\n\nPatient: I... I know you just asked me this, and I really want to give you the answer you're looking for. I want to say I know for certain it's just my brain. I know that's what I'm supposed to say, and I want to be doing this right. \n\nBut... oh, please don't be upset with me... I still think it's really them. I know I'm probably frustrating you, and I'm so sorry. I just... I can't shake the feeling that they're really there, talking through the walls and the phone. It just feels too real. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's the wrong answer again. I'm trying so hard to be a good patient, I really am. Am I doing okay, despite this? I promise I'll keep working on it."
  }
